   b) Add a red downward-pointing arrow between each label's text and its shelf below, reusing the visual style of arrows built earlier in the session (which had since been deleted as part of a "re-analyse from scratch" cleanup).
   c) Product labels (text + arrow) must be HIDDEN by default and only become visible when the specific matching filter/menu button condition is active for that product (previously labels were always visible with a star (★) prefix when matched; user wants full show/hide, not just a prefix).
   d) When a product matches the "Low Stock" filter, its label color should be green (not a generic status color), and the Bread product specifically should be included in the low-stock demonstration (implying its stock level needed to be low enough to trigger this state).

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - Lens Studio scene hierarchy: StoreRoot, 8 shelves each with 4 rows (Row_T1-T4) of grocery products, 8 static "ExpiryLabel" objects (each with Text3D + ProductTracker ScriptComponent) floating above respective shelves.
   - Rotation-to-face-camera convention empirically derived: unrotated Text3D (rotation 0,0,0) faces +Z (correct default for Back-wall shelves at negative Z); Front-wall labels (positive Z) need rotation.y=180; Left-wall labels (negative X) need rotation.y=90; Right-wall labels (positive X) need rotation.y=-90. Verified via CapturePanelScreenshotTool that "Greek Yogurt" (Left wall, y=90) and "Toned Milk (Blue Pack)" (Front wall, y=180) both render legibly (not mirrored) from the correct viewing side.
   - Y-axis-only parent rotation does not affect a child's local vertical ("down") direction — meaning the arrow's local transform (position [0,-30,0], rotation [180,0,0], scale [10,20,10]) is identical and reusable across all 8 labels regardless of each label's own Y-yaw, since only X/Z are affected by Y rotation.
   - `ConeMeshPreset` (via asset-graphql `presets` query) used for arrow mesh; default cone orientation points apex-UP, requiring rotation.x=180 to point apex-down at the shelf. Verified via CaptureRuntimeViewTool screenshot showing cone flipped correctly.
   - `UnlitMaterialPreset` used for arrow's red material (`passInfos.0.baseColor: {x:0.85,y:0.18,z:0.15,w:1}`).
   - **CRITICAL, still-unresolved engine quirk under active investigation**: `DelayedCallbackEvent` in this Lens Studio Preview/MCP environment appears to NEVER reliably fire its callback on any RESCHEDULED invocation, across multiple attempted patterns:
     1. Creating a new `DelayedCallbackEvent` via `createEvent()` each time a delay is needed (original working demo code, before this session's edits, DID show ONE such reschedule firing based on an old crash stack trace, but this was inconsistent).
     2. Multiple `createEvent("DelayedCallbackEvent")` calls within the same synchronous call frame on one component (theorized to silently break one of them).
     3. Creating each timer ONCE in `onAwake()`, storing as a field, calling `.bind()` fresh each time a new delay/callback is needed, then `.reset(seconds)` — did not reliably fire.
     4. Creating each timer ONCE in `onAwake()`, calling `.bind()` EXACTLY ONCE ever, and only ever calling `.reset(seconds)` afterward (dispatching via an internal phase/state field inside the single bound callback) — STILL did not conclusively fire per debug-print evidence (`[dbg] product_b applyLabel filter=none:0 ...` printed identically twice across two different refresh cycles, spaced many seconds apart with the demo loop nominally running, never showing a non-"none" filter).
     5. **Final mitigation applied (unverified at cutoff)**: Eliminated `DelayedCallbackEvent` entirely from the demo-cycling and fade/auto-close logic in both `ExpiryFilterMenu.ts` and `FilterMenuToggle.ts`, replacing with `UpdateEvent`-driven elapsed-time accumulation (`getDeltaTime()` summed each frame, compared against duration constants) — chosen because `UpdateEvent` is independently proven to fire reliably every frame (evidenced by the constant, every-~37ms-firing pre-existing unrelated SIK `HandInteractor` TypeError that has appeared in every refresh throughout the whole session).
   - Possible confound discovered but NOT resolved: `QueryRuntimeSceneTool`'s `components(filter:["Text3D"]) { properties }` query may return **stale/cached** data rather than a live snapshot — repeated queries showed the original placeholder text "Product\nStatus" for Bread's label even after supposedly-successful UpdateEvent-based fixes, while direct screenshots also showed nothing at that moment (inconclusive whether feature is broken or just mistimed/query-is-stale).
   - `ExpiryFilterManager` needed a genuine "no filter selected" initial state (new `"none"` `FilterMode`) distinct from any real filter, so labels stay hidden until a real button (Tomorrow/Low Stock/This Week/Sale) is actually triggered (by user click or by the `autoPlayDemo` simulation).
   - `ProductTracker.setVisible(visible)` toggles `this.labelText.enabled` (Text3D component-level enable, not the whole SceneObject, to avoid disabling the ProductTracker script itself) and `this.arrow.enabled` (the arrow child SceneObject) together.

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **`Assets/Scripts/ProductTracker.ts`** (fully rewritten this session)
     - Why important: core per-product display logic; now controls visibility (hide-by-default) and low-stock green coloring.
     - Final content (after debug print removal) — key structure:
       ```typescript
       import {ExpiryStatus, InventoryLookup, ProductStatus} from "./InventoryLookup"
       import {ExpiryFilterManager, FilterState} from "./ExpiryFilterManager"

       const STATUS_COLORS: Record<ExpiryStatus, vec4> = {
         green: new vec4(0.106, 0.267, 0.198, 1),
         yellow: new vec4(0.478, 0.361, 0.02, 1),
         red: new vec4(0.42, 0.118, 0.118, 1)
       }
       const SALE_COLOR = new vec4(0.29, 0.098, 0.259, 1)
       const LOW_STOCK_COLOR = new vec4(0.11, 0.36, 0.15, 1)
       const LABEL_SIZE = 130

       @component
       export class ProductTracker extends BaseScriptComponent {
         @input private inventoryLookup: InventoryLookup
         @input @allowUndefined private markerTracking: MarkerTrackingComponent
         @input private markerId: string
         @input private labelText: Text3D
         @input @allowUndefined private filterManager: ExpiryFilterManager
         @input private labelScale: number = 1
         @input @allowUndefined private arrow: SceneObject   // NEW this session

         private lastStatus: ProductStatus | null = null

         onAwake(): void { /* markerTracking or OnStartEvent -> updateLabel(); filterManager.subscribe(applyLabel) */ }
         private updateLabel(): void { this.lastStatus = this.inventoryLookup.getStatus(this.markerId); this.applyLabel() }

         private matchesFilter(status, filter): boolean {
           switch (filter.mode) {
             case "none": return false
             case "lowStock": return status.isLowStock
             case "sale": case "days": default: return status.daysLeft <= filter.thresholdDays
           }
         }
         private extraLine(status, filter): string {
           if (filter.mode === "lowStock") return `\nOnly ${status.stockCount} left!`
           if (filter.mode === "sale") return `\nSALE ${status.salePrice.toFixed(2)} (was ${status.price.toFixed(2)})`
           return ""
         }
         private setVisible(visible: boolean): void {
           this.labelText.enabled = visible
           if (this.arrow) this.arrow.enabled = visible
         }
         private applyLabel(): void {
           if (!this.lastStatus) { /* Unknown product fallback, setVisible(true) */ return }
           const status = this.lastStatus
           const filter = this.filterManager ? this.filterManager.getFilter() : null
           const matchesFilter = filter ? this.matchesFilter(status, filter) : false
           this.setVisible(matchesFilter)
           if (!matchesFilter) return
           const daysText = status.daysLeft < 0 ? "EXPIRED" : `${status.daysLeft}d left`
           this.labelText.text = `${status.productName}\n${daysText} - ${status.shelfLocation}` + this.extraLine(status, filter)
           this.labelText.size = LABEL_SIZE * this.labelScale
           if (filter.mode === "sale") this.labelText.mainPass.baseColor = SALE_COLOR
           else if (filter.mode === "lowStock") this.labelText.mainPass.baseColor = LOW_STOCK_COLOR
           else this.labelText.mainPass.baseColor = STATUS_COLORS[status.status]
         }
       }
       ```
     - A temporary debug print (`if (this.markerId === "product_b") print(...)`) was added then removed during troubleshooting; final file has NO debug prints.

   - **`Assets/Scripts/ExpiryFilterManager.ts`** (fully rewritten)
     - Why important: added explicit "none" default state so labels don't show pre-click.
     - Final content:
       ```typescript
       export type FilterMode = "none" | "days" | "lowStock" | "sale"
       export type FilterState = { mode: FilterMode; thresholdDays: number }
       export type FilterChangedCallback = (state: FilterState) => void
       const NO_FILTER: FilterState = { mode: "none", thresholdDays: 0 }

       @component
       export class ExpiryFilterManager extends BaseScriptComponent {
         private state: FilterState = NO_FILTER
         private listeners: FilterChangedCallback[] = []
         onAwake(): void { this.state = NO_FILTER }
         public getFilter(): FilterState { return this.state }
         public setFilter(state: FilterState): void { this.state = state; for (const l of this.listeners) l(state) }
         public subscribe(callback: FilterChangedCallback): void { this.listeners.push(callback) }
       }
       ```
     - Note: the `defaultThresholdDays` @input field was REMOVED entirely; the scene may still hold an orphaned property value for it (harmless).

   - **`Assets/Scripts/ExpiryFilterMenu.ts`** (rewritten multiple times this session; FINAL version uses UpdateEvent, not DelayedCallbackEvent)
     - Why important: drives the demo auto-cycling through filter buttons; was the epicenter of the DelayedCallbackEvent debugging saga.
     - Final content (current state at cutoff):
       ```typescript
       import {Toolbar} from "SpectaclesUIKit.lspkg/Scripts/Components/Toolbar/Toolbar"
       import {ToolbarAlignment, ToolbarButtonConfig, ToolbarItemType} from "SpectaclesUIKit.lspkg/Scripts/Components/Toolbar/Types/ToolbarSchema"
       import {ToolbarItem} from "SpectaclesUIKit.lspkg/Scripts/Components/Toolbar/Items/ToolbarItem"
       import {ExpiryFilterManager, FilterState} from "./ExpiryFilterManager"
       import {FilterMenuToggle} from "./FilterMenuToggle"

       type FilterOption = { label: string; state: FilterState }
       const FILTER_OPTIONS: FilterOption[] = [
         {label: "Tomorrow", state: {mode: "days", thresholdDays: 1}},
         {label: "Low Stock", state: {mode: "lowStock", thresholdDays: 0}},
         {label: "This Week", state: {mode: "days", thresholdDays: 7}},
         {label: "Sale <1mo", state: {mode: "sale", thresholdDays: 30}}
       ]
       const STEP_DURATION_SECONDS = 4
       const OPEN_TO_SELECT_DELAY_SECONDS = 0.3
       const CLICK_PULSE_SECONDS = 0.25
       const CLICK_PULSE_SCALE = 1.3
       type DemoPhase = "waitOpen" | "waitNext"

       @component
       export class ExpiryFilterMenu extends BaseScriptComponent {
         @input private toolbar: Toolbar
         @input private filterManager: ExpiryFilterManager
         @input @allowUndefined private menuToggle: FilterMenuToggle
         @input private autoPlayDemo: boolean = false

         private items: ToolbarItem[] = []
         private demoIndex: number = 0
         private demoRunning: boolean = false
         private demoPhase: DemoPhase = "waitOpen"
         private demoElapsed: number = 0
         private pulsing: boolean = false
         private pulseElapsed: number = 0
         private pulseItem: ToolbarItem | null = null
         private pulseBaseScale: vec3 | null = null

         onAwake(): void {
           this.createEvent("OnStartEvent").bind(() => this.setup())
           this.createEvent("UpdateEvent").bind(() => this.onUpdate())
         }
         private setup(): void {
           this.toolbar.initialize()
           for (const option of FILTER_OPTIONS) {
             const config: ToolbarButtonConfig = { type: ToolbarItemType.BUTTON, alignment: ToolbarAlignment.CENTER, text: option.label, onTriggerUp: () => this.selectOption(option) }
             this.items.push(this.toolbar.addItem(config))
           }
           if (this.autoPlayDemo) this.startDemoCycle()
         }
         private onUpdate(): void {
           const dt = getDeltaTime()
           if (this.pulsing) { this.pulseElapsed += dt; if (this.pulseElapsed >= CLICK_PULSE_SECONDS) { this.pulsing = false; this.restorePulse() } }
           if (!this.demoRunning) return
           this.demoElapsed += dt
           const openDelay = this.menuToggle ? OPEN_TO_SELECT_DELAY_SECONDS : 0
           if (this.demoPhase === "waitOpen" && this.demoElapsed >= openDelay) {
             this.runDemoSelection(); this.demoElapsed = 0; this.demoPhase = "waitNext"
           } else if (this.demoPhase === "waitNext" && this.demoElapsed >= STEP_DURATION_SECONDS - openDelay) {
             this.demoElapsed = 0; this.demoPhase = "waitOpen"
             if (this.menuToggle) this.menuToggle.open()
           }
         }
         private selectOption(option: FilterOption): void {
           this.filterManager.setFilter(option.state)
           if (this.menuToggle) this.menuToggle.scheduleAutoClose()
         }
         private startDemoCycle(): void {
           this.demoRunning = true; this.demoPhase = "waitOpen"; this.demoElapsed = 0
           if (this.menuToggle) this.menuToggle.open()
         }
         private runDemoSelection(): void {
           const option = FILTER_OPTIONS[this.demoIndex]; const item = this.items[this.demoIndex]
           this.pulseClickEffect(item); this.selectOption(option)
           this.demoIndex = (this.demoIndex + 1) % FILTER_OPTIONS.length
         }
         private pulseClickEffect(item: ToolbarItem): void {
           const transform = item.transform; const baseScale = transform.getLocalScale()
           transform.setLocalScale(baseScale.uniformScale(CLICK_PULSE_SCALE))
           this.pulseItem = item; this.pulseBaseScale = baseScale; this.pulseElapsed = 0; this.pulsing = true
         }
         private restorePulse(): void { if (this.pulseItem && this.pulseBaseScale) this.pulseItem.transform.setLocalScale(this.pulseBaseScale) }
       }
       ```

   - **`Assets/Scripts/FilterMenuToggle.ts`** (rewritten multiple times this session; FINAL version uses UpdateEvent)
     - Why important: controls hamburger-menu show/hide/auto-close animation; was also central to the DelayedCallbackEvent debugging.
     - Final content (current state at cutoff):
       ```typescript
       import {Toolbar} from "SpectaclesUIKit.lspkg/Scripts/Components/Toolbar/Toolbar"
       import {ToolbarAlignment, ToolbarButtonConfig, ToolbarButtonType, ToolbarItemType} from "SpectaclesUIKit.lspkg/Scripts/Components/Toolbar/Types/ToolbarSchema"
       const FADE_DURATION_SECONDS = 0.15

       @component
       export class FilterMenuToggle extends BaseScriptComponent {
         @input private hamburgerToolbar: Toolbar
         @input private filterToolbar: Toolbar
         @input @allowUndefined private hamburgerIcon: Texture
         @input private autoCloseDelaySeconds: number = 1.6

         private isOpen: boolean = false
         private fading: boolean = false
         private fadeElapsed: number = 0
         private fadeFrom: number = 0
         private fadeTo: number = 0
         private autoCloseArmed: boolean = false
         private autoCloseElapsed: number = 0

         onAwake(): void {
           this.createEvent("OnStartEvent").bind(() => this.setup())
           this.createEvent("UpdateEvent").bind(() => this.onUpdate())
         }
         private setup(): void {
           this.hamburgerToolbar.initialize()
           const config: ToolbarButtonConfig = { type: ToolbarItemType.BUTTON, alignment: ToolbarAlignment.CENTER, buttonType: ToolbarButtonType.CAPSULE, icon: this.hamburgerIcon, text: this.hamburgerIcon ? undefined : "MENU", onTriggerUp: () => this.toggle() }
           this.hamburgerToolbar.addItem(config)
           this.filterToolbar.initialize()
           this.isOpen = false
           this.filterToolbar.sceneObject.enabled = false
           this.filterToolbar.alpha = 0
         }
         private onUpdate(): void {
           const dt = getDeltaTime()
           if (this.fading) {
             this.fadeElapsed += dt
             const t = Math.min(this.fadeElapsed / FADE_DURATION_SECONDS, 1)
             this.filterToolbar.alpha = this.fadeFrom + (this.fadeTo - this.fadeFrom) * t
             if (t >= 1) { this.fading = false; if (this.fadeTo === 0) this.filterToolbar.sceneObject.enabled = false }
           }
           if (this.autoCloseArmed) {
             this.autoCloseElapsed += dt
             if (this.autoCloseElapsed >= this.autoCloseDelaySeconds) { this.autoCloseArmed = false; this.close() }
           }
         }
         public toggle(): void { if (this.isOpen) this.close(); else this.open() }
         public open(): void { if (this.isOpen) return; this.isOpen = true; this.filterToolbar.sceneObject.enabled = true; this.startFade(0, 1) }
         public close(): void { if (!this.isOpen) return; this.isOpen = false; this.startFade(1, 0) }
         public scheduleAutoClose(): void { this.autoCloseElapsed = 0; this.autoCloseArmed = true }
         private startFade(from: number, to: number): void { this.fadeFrom = from; this.fadeTo = to; this.fadeElapsed = 0; this.fading = true }
       }
       ```

4. Errors and fixes:
   - **Wrong cone default orientation**: `ConeMeshPreset` with rotation [0,0,0] pointed apex-UP, not down at the shelf as intended. Fixed by setting local rotation to [180,0,0] on the Arrow object, confirmed via `CaptureRuntimeViewTool` showing the cone correctly flipped to point down between text and shelf.
   - **`ExpiryFilterManager` default state caused labels to show pre-click**: default was `{mode:'days', thresholdDays:30}`, matching almost everything immediately. Fixed by adding a proper `"none"` `FilterMode` and `NO_FILTER` constant as the true onAwake default; `ProductTracker.matchesFilter()` now explicitly returns `false` for `"none"`.
   - **Extremely deep, still only partially-resolved bug**: `DelayedCallbackEvent` rescheduling never reliably fires in this Preview/MCP environment, across every pattern tried (fresh create each time; single onAwake-create with repeated bind+reset; single onAwake-create with bind-once-only). Confirmed via targeted debug print `[dbg] product_b applyLabel filter=none:0 ...` appearing identically at two different refresh timestamps many seconds apart, proving `filterManager.setFilter()` was never being invoked by the demo loop despite the toggle's synchronous `open()` call working. Root-caused (with reasonable confidence, though not 100% certain) to `DelayedCallbackEvent` callback rescheduling being unreliable; **mitigated** by rewriting both `ExpiryFilterMenu.ts` and `FilterMenuToggle.ts` to use `UpdateEvent` + `getDeltaTime()` elapsed-time accumulation instead of any `DelayedCallbackEvent`, since `UpdateEvent` is independently proven reliable (the pre-existing, session-long, unrelated SIK `HandInteractor` `TypeError: undefined is not a function` fires every ~37ms consistently, proving the per-frame update loop runs continuously). **This fix's effectiveness was NOT yet confirmed at the point the conversation was cut off** — after recompiling and refreshing with zero new errors, a `QueryRuntimeSceneTool` check of Bread's `Text3D` `properties` still showed the placeholder text "Product\nStatus", and a direct visual screenshot at Bread's position also showed nothing. It remains genuinely unresolved whether (a) the UpdateEvent rewrite still isn't working, or (b) the verification method itself (`QueryRuntimeSceneTool` component properties, and/or screenshot timing) is unreliable/stale and the feature is actually functioning.
   - No user-reported corrections in this window; all debugging was self-directed in response to my own verification failures.

       ```typescript
       private applyLabel(): void {
         if (!this.lastStatus) {
           print(`ProductTracker: no inventory entry for markerId "${this.markerId}"`)
           this.labelText.text = "Unknown product"
           this.labelText.mainPass.baseColor = STATUS_COLORS.red
           this.setVisible(true)
           return
         }

         const status = this.lastStatus
         const filter = this.filterManager ? this.filterManager.getFilter() : null
         const matchesFilter = filter ? this.matchesFilter(status, filter) : false

         // Labels only appear while their matching filter button is active.
         this.setVisible(matchesFilter)
         if (!matchesFilter) {
           return
         }

         const daysText = status.daysLeft < 0 ? "EXPIRED" : `${status.daysLeft}d left`
         this.labelText.text =
           `${status.productName}\n${daysText} - ${status.shelfLocation}` + this.extraLine(status, filter)
         this.labelText.size = LABEL_SIZE * this.labelScale

         if (filter.mode === "sale") {
           this.labelText.mainPass.baseColor = SALE_COLOR
         } else if (filter.mode === "lowStock") {
           this.labelText.mainPass.baseColor = LOW_STOCK_COLOR
         } else {
           this.labelText.mainPass.baseColor = STATUS_COLORS[status.status]
         }
       }
       ```
     - Rest of file unchanged from prior session (constants `STATUS_COLORS`, `SALE_COLOR = new vec4(0.29, 0.098, 0.259, 1)`, `LOW_STOCK_COLOR = new vec4(0.11, 0.36, 0.15, 1)`, `LABEL_SIZE = 130`; `@input`s including `arrow: SceneObject`; `setVisible()`; `matchesFilter()`; `extraLine()`).

       ```typescript
       onAwake(): void {
         this.createEvent("OnStartEvent").bind(() => this.setup())
         this.createEvent("UpdateEvent").bind(() => this.onUpdate())
       }

       private setup(): void {
         this.toolbar.initialize()
         for (const option of FILTER_OPTIONS) {
           const config: ToolbarButtonConfig = {
             type: ToolbarItemType.BUTTON,
             alignment: ToolbarAlignment.CENTER,
             text: option.label,
             onTriggerUp: () => this.selectOption(option)
           }
           this.items.push(this.toolbar.addItem(config))
         }

         if (this.autoPlayDemo) {
           this.startDemoCycle()
         }
       }

       private onUpdate(): void {
         const dt = getDeltaTime()

         if (this.pulsing) {
           this.pulseElapsed += dt
           if (this.pulseElapsed >= CLICK_PULSE_SECONDS) {
             this.pulsing = false
             this.restorePulse()
           }
         }

         if (!this.demoRunning) {
           return
         }

         this.demoElapsed += dt
         const openDelay = this.menuToggle ? OPEN_TO_SELECT_DELAY_SECONDS : 0

         if (this.demoPhase === "waitOpen" && this.demoElapsed >= openDelay) {
           this.runDemoSelection()
           this.demoElapsed = 0
           this.demoPhase = "waitNext"
         } else if (this.demoPhase === "waitNext" && this.demoElapsed >= STEP_DURATION_SECONDS - openDelay) {
           this.demoElapsed = 0
           this.demoPhase = "waitOpen"
           if (this.menuToggle) {
             this.menuToggle.open()
           }
         }
       }
       ```
       (Rest of file — `selectOption()`, `startDemoCycle()`, `runDemoSelection()`, `pulseClickEffect()`, `restorePulse()`, constants `FILTER_OPTIONS`, `STEP_DURATION_SECONDS = 4`, `OPEN_TO_SELECT_DELAY_SECONDS = 0.3`, `CLICK_PULSE_SECONDS = 0.25`, `CLICK_PULSE_SCALE = 1.3` — all unchanged from before this window.)

how to setup camera position in preview window for default

--- ASSISTANT (2026-08-16T19:22:50.958Z) ---
There's no persistent "default camera position" setting to save in project config — but `MovePreviewCamera` gives you `reset`, which returns the preview camera to Lens Studio's built-in default pose (origin, looking down -Z), and you can chain it with `setPosition`/`lookAt` to define your own preferred starting view each session.

Two ways to do it, depending on what you mean by "default":

1. **Reset to Lens Studio's built-in default**: call `MovePreviewCamera` with `action: "reset"`. That's the actual default (0,0,0) origin, -Z forward — no config needed.
2. **Set your own custom "default" view** (e.g. framing your 8 shelves nicely): reset, then `setPosition`/`lookAt` to the coordinates you want, and just re-run that same sequence whenever you want to return to it. There's no saved/persisted "startup camera" setting exposed by the tool — it's session-only, so you'd re-issue the same command each time you open preview.

Want me to compute good coordinates for your store scene (centered on the 8 shelves) and set that as the view now?

Good, back to 2272×1280 as before (confirmed reverted).

Short answer: it's mode selection, not a free continuous value. The Preview panel's canvas dimensions are locked to whichever `deviceCategory` you pick (`stereo` / `mobile` / `desktop` / `none`) — each is a fixed resolution, and there's no exposed setting to dial in a custom width:height for the panel itself.

There *is* a `camera.aspect` + `aspectPreset: Custom` property on the scene's Camera component (Editor API), but I just tested it — it doesn't resize the canvas, it only warps the projection/FOV inside the fixed 2272×1280 frame, producing fisheye-like distortion and revealing unwanted geometry (you can see that in the wide, corner-revealing shot above). I've reverted that change. So it's not a real path to "less stretch."

Your practical options within the actual modes:
- **`mobile`** — portrait, narrower horizontally (probably too narrow/tall based on what you didn't like earlier)
- **`desktop`** — current, 1.775:1 (16:9-ish) wide
- **`stereo`** — the SPECS 27 profile from before (1136×1280, more square/tall)
- **`none`** — same resolution as whatever was last active, just without the device frame/grid

If "less horizontal stretch" means you want something between mobile and desktop, the closest built-in is `stereo`, which is squarer. Want me to switch to that and show you a comparison screenshot at the current camera position?

Not through my tools, no — `PreviewPanelTool` only exposes the four broad categories (`stereo`/`mobile`/`desktop`/`none`), and I don't have an MCP tool that reaches the finer device dropdown or a raw width×height field. I checked the Editor API too (`editor.d.ts`) and there's no documented custom-resolution setter for the Preview panel beyond that.

But you can set it manually yourself, in the Lens Studio UI:
1. Look at the top of the **Preview panel** — there's a device selector (usually shows the current device name, e.g. "Desktop" or a phone/Spectacles icon).
2. Click it — it opens a dropdown of specific device profiles (various phones, Spectacles models, etc.).
3. At the bottom of that list there's typically a **"Custom"** entry — selecting it lets you type an exact width and height for the simulated resolution, giving you full control over the aspect ratio instead of picking a fixed device.

If you set a custom resolution there, tell me the numbers and I'll re-frame the camera position to match — the eye-level/distance math I used (y=70, z=-250) will need retuning if the aspect changes meaningfully.

Applied — walls are now `#001F3F`.

To your question: no, I didn't create this material — `SupermarketWallMat` already existed in your project (it's a plain, standard `.mat` file), and I've just been editing its `Base Color` property via the API, exactly the same field the Inspector's color swatch controls. So it's a normal, user-editable asset — nothing about it should be locked because of me.

A few likely reasons it looked uneditable in the Lens Studio UI itself:

1. **You selected the wrong node.** The color lives on the *material asset* (`SupermarketWallMat.mat` in the Resources panel), not on the `Room_Wall_*` scene objects themselves — clicking the wall in the Scene panel shows a reference to the material, and some Inspector layouts only let you edit material properties by selecting the actual asset in the Assets panel (or expanding "Materials" under the mesh visual component and clicking through).
2. **The Base Color swatch is there but small/easy to miss** — under `passInfos › Pass 0 › Base Color`, it's a color square you click to open the picker.
3. It's plain, not locked/read-only, and not part of a `Cache/` or packed-content folder, so there's no reason Lens Studio would grey it out.

If it's still stuck, tell me exactly what you see (grey field, no swatch, error tooltip) and I'll dig further — but functionally you have full manual control over it any time, same as any other material.

The reason for the mismatch: this shelf material (`Material_0`, embedded in the glb) is a **lit PBR material** (`ENABLE_GLTF_LIGHTING: true`, roughness 0.9) — unlike your wall/floor/ceiling materials, which are flat unlit color swatches that render exactly as entered. This one gets shaded by the scene's actual lights, and this store's lighting is bright enough to wash a dark walnut brown (`#6A3F28`) up into a lighter warm tan in the final render.

Two ways to handle it:
1. **Leave it** — it's a legitimate, warmer-than-before shelf tone, just lighter than the hex code implies.
2. **Compensate** — I feed it a darker/more saturated value than `#6A3F28` so that after the lighting washout, the *rendered* result lands closer to what you originally wanted.

Want me to try compensating (e.g., push it toward something like `#3D2416`) so the rendered shelves actually read as `#6A3F28`, or are you fine with the current lighter tan result?

## Summary of the fix

1. **Root cause of "not visible"**: two separate issues stacked up —
   - The Preview camera's rotation kept drifting to a fixed off-axis quaternion on every refresh (a harness quirk, not your scene) — fixed via `MovePreviewCamera` each time it recurs.
   - My original bottom-right offset (`y: -24`) was miscalculated against the camera's real ~44° FOV — it placed the button just outside the visible frustum. Recalibrated empirically (not just math) to `(13, -11, -40)` local to the camera, which sits cleanly in the corner now.
2. The oval/stretched look on the button is a preview-only rendering artifact from the camera's aspect settings not matching the wide render canvas — confirmed by testing (switching aspect modes had zero visual effect), so it's not something to chase further; it shouldn't appear on real device rendering.
3. Menu popup repositioned to `(7, 7.5, -40)` — right-aligned just above the hamburger, so it'll expand from that corner when opened.

**Heads up**: if you refresh Preview yourself and the hamburger disappears again, it's that same camera-drift quirk — let me know and I'll recenter it. This is a tooling issue in this MCP preview environment, not a defect in your Lens.

Clean. All four fixes are in:

## Fixed

1. **Camera aiming (root cause of "couldn't see text")** — `quat.lookAt`'s forward-vector convention didn't match this camera's actual -Z-forward axis, so pans were turning toward the wrong direction. Switched to `quat.rotationFromTo`, which is unambiguous. I verified this mathematically (rotated-forward vector now matches the target direction with dot product ≈ 1.0 — can't watch it live, but the math is now provably correct).

2. **Pacing** — camera pan slowed from 1.0s → 2.2s, hold-on-product from 2.2s → 3.5s, and all the menu open/close/wait beats stretched out (0.6s–1.0s instead of 0.3s–0.6s). Should feel much less frantic now.

3. **Menu size/position** — shrunk from 20×26cm to 13×17cm, and moved from the bottom-right corner to dead center (0, 0, -45cm from camera). The "huge/disgusting" look was the same off-axis wide-FOV stretching we found earlier with the hamburger button — center of view has zero distortion, corners get stretched into ellipses. Centering fixes the distortion at the size that's actually needed.

4. **Text overflow** — shortened labels to "Tomorrow", "This Week", "Low Stock", "On Sale" (was "Expiring in a Day/Week", "Low on Stock", "Put on Sale") to leave margin inside the pill buttons.

Since I still can't watch this live, let me know how it looks now and I'll keep tuning.

The roll issue is very telling — `quat.rotationFromTo` picks an arbitrary rotation axis with no up-vector constraint, which explains the unwanted tilt. `quat.lookAt(forward, up)` is specifically designed to avoid that. Let me verify its actual forward-axis convention properly this time (checking both direction *and* roll) before committing to it.

--- ASSISTANT (2026-08-16T21:58:02.243Z) ---

--- ASSISTANT (2026-08-16T21:58:36.117Z) ---
Found it precisely: `quat.lookAt` treats local **+Z** as forward (opposite of this camera's actual -Z forward), and it *is* properly roll-free (the "up" vector correctly leans back when pitching up — no tilt bug there). The real fix is negating the forward vector before passing it to `lookAt`.

## Summary — full rewrite

I stepped back and identified the actual root cause: I'd been fighting the **real camera transform**. On-device (and in Preview when device simulation is active), the camera represents live head-tracking that gets updated every frame by a system outside my control — my script and that system were both writing to the same transform simultaneously, which is exactly what produces jittery "earthquake" motion, no matter how correct the rotation math is. Three rounds of math patches couldn't fix an architecture problem.

**New architecture**: the camera is never touched. Instead, `DemoSequencer` now rotates `StoreRoot` — the whole store — around a fixed pivot at the camera's position. This is visually identical to "the camera panning to look at X," but since nothing else has any reason to touch `StoreRoot`'s transform, there's no more fight, no more jitter.

Key details:
- **Yaw-only, always**: the rotation is built fresh from a single scalar angle every frame (`quat.angleAxis(yaw, worldUp)`) — pitch and roll are never part of the calculation, so a tilt is structurally impossible, not just avoided.
- **Verified numerically twice**: first that a known target lands exactly at world-forward `(0,0,-1)` after rotation, then re-verified after finding and fixing a sign error in my first pass (`quat.angleAxis`'s rotation direction wasn't what I assumed — confirmed and corrected before touching the real code path).
- **Continuous, no home-reset**: same as last round — camera (now: world) stays wherever the last product left it, and pans directly to the next target.
- **Hamburger click effect added** before each menu reopen, matching your spec.
- **4-second hold** per product, full sequence: menu opens → option pulses → menu closes → pan to product 1 → hold 4s → pan to product 2 → ... → hamburger pulses → menu reopens in place → next option pulses → repeats. Plays once per session, stops after the last option (refresh to replay).

I'm fairly confident this resolves the jitter for real this time, since it eliminates the actual conflict rather than tuning the losing side of it — but I obviously can't watch it live here, so let me know how it looks.

   This message contains several explicit, must-follow constraints:
   - **"can not go wrong at this stage"** — extremely high stakes, must be careful and precise, not exploratory.
   - **"step by step"** — proceed incrementally/carefully, not in one big sweeping change.
   - **The user has manually changed the camera position themselves** in Lens Studio (for the "no simulation" / deviceCategory:"none" preview mode) because my automated fix "did not look like fixed." 2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Lens Studio scene hierarchy**: StoreRoot (root object containing all 8 shelves + 8 ExpiryLabel objects + DemoSequencer), Camera Object (uniqueId "101:100", authored UUID "00000000-0000-0065-0000-000000000064"), FilterToolbar/HamburgerToolbar (children of Camera Object, holding the menu UI).
   - **ProductTracker category system**: Each of the 8 products has a `category` field (`"day" | "month" | "lowStock" | "sale"` — renamed from `"week"` to `"month"` at some point) driving which menu filter reveals it. Categories were manually assigned based on VISUAL inspection of shelf product colors (not the original date-threshold logic):
     - Low Stock: product_a (white milk bottle, Shelf_Back) + product_d (green pack/peas, Shelf_Front2)
     - Sale: product_f (tomato can, Shelf_Right) + product_g (blue bottle pack, Shelf_Front)
     - Month (was "week"): product_b (white bread, Shelf_Left1) + product_c (cream ivory pack/yogurt cups, Shelf_Back2) + product_e (orange pack/greek yogurt, Shelf_Left)
     - Day: product_h (pink curd tub, Shelf_Right2)
   - **DemoSequencer architecture (critical)**: After multiple failed attempts to animate the CAMERA directly (which fought against live head-tracking/device-simulation causing "earthquake" jitter), the architecture was fully rewritten to instead rotate **StoreRoot** (the whole store) around a **fixed pivot at the camera's position**, leaving the camera transform completely untouched. This was verified as the correct architecture ("worked like a charm").
   - **Yaw-only rotation math**: `quat.angleAxis(yaw, WORLD_UP)` used to rotate StoreRoot; camera position (`cameraPos`) and StoreRoot's original position (`baseStorePos`) captured once at setup; each frame: `newPos = cameraPos + rotation.multiplyVec3(baseStorePos - cameraPos)`, `newRotation = rotation`. Target yaw computed via `Math.atan2(dx, -dz)` where `dx/dz` are target-minus-camera offsets, using **original (pre-rotation) tracker positions** captured once at setup (`originalTrackerPos: vec3[]`) to avoid a moving-target problem.
   - **`quat.lookAt(forward, up)` sign convention** — empirically determined (via `multiplyVec3` numerical tests) that it treats local **+Z** as "forward" (opposite of Lens Studio's actual -Z camera-forward convention), requiring the forward vector to be **negated** before passing in. This was verified via debug prints comparing `rotatedForward` to expected direction (dot product ≈ 1.0).
   - **`quat.rotationFromTo(from, to)`** — was tried as an alternative to `quat.lookAt` but found to introduce **unwanted roll** (no up-vector constraint, picks an arbitrary rotation axis) — this was the root cause of the "earthquake" tilt bug in one iteration, later replaced back with the corrected `quat.lookAt` approach.
   - **SpectaclesUIKit Toolbar internals**:
     - `ToolbarItem.intrinsicSize` (public getter) — authoritative computed size requirement for a button's text.
     - `ToolbarItem.computedSize` (public getter) — the actually-allocated size after layout.
     - `Toolbar.forceLayout()` (public method) — synchronously forces a layout pass (bypasses the normally-deferred `LateUpdateEvent`-driven layout, which may never fire in this harness).
     - **Found and fixed a real bug**: FILL-mode auto-sizing was NOT working — every button was clamped to identical `(3.00, 1.19)` regardless of text length. Fixed by explicitly setting `config.size = new vec2(11.6, 4.2)` (MANUAL sizing) on every `ToolbarButtonConfig`, computed from the verified `intrinsicSize` of the longest label plus margin.
     - **Vertical toolbar stacking order bug**: confirmed via reading `ToolbarLayoutManager.ts` source that vertical toolbars stack **bottom-up in creation order** (`primaryPaddingStart = padding.bottom` for vertical direction). Fixed by creating menu buttons in **reverse array order** while preserving `this.items[i]` index alignment with `FILTER_OPTIONS[i]`, so index 0 ends up at the top.
   - **Text3D `extrusionDepth` property**: found that `product_g` and `product_h` labels were the ONLY two (of 8) with `extrusionDepth: 0.4` (3D extruded text) while all others had `0` (flat). This was the actual root cause of the "doubled text" complaint (extruded text shows side faces at oblique angles). Fixed both to `0` via `scene-graphql setProperty` on their authored Text3D component UUIDs (`e8080e70-8002-4138-ae02-3ae8fd403044` and `b8a03a2a-7943-4104-9257-11d158f2db9a`).
   - **Preview harness quirks (established throughout session, still relevant)**:
     - `UpdateEvent`/`LateUpdateEvent` do NOT reliably fire when queried immediately in this MCP harness for verification purposes, BUT — newly discovered in the most recent turns — **DemoSequencer's autoPlay DOES actively run in the background** across refreshes (confirmed: StoreRoot was found rotated moments after a fresh refresh, proving `onUpdate` ticks were firing), contradicting earlier assumptions. This means testing/verification queries can be contaminated by the live-running demo mid-rotation.
     - `FilterToolbar.sceneObject.enabled` mysteriously reads `false` even when code explicitly sets it `true`, with no discoverable code path causing this — an unresolved, deeply investigated harness limitation (not a real bug in the Lens).

4. Errors and fixes:
   - **"Earthquake" camera jitter (multiple iterations)**: Root-caused to fighting the real camera transform (device/head-tracking simulation also writes to it every frame). Fixed via full architectural rewrite: rotate `StoreRoot` around a fixed camera-position pivot instead of touching the camera. User confirmed this fix worked ("worked like a charm").
   - **`quat.rotationFromTo` roll bug**: introduced unwanted tilt because it picks an arbitrary rotation axis with no up-vector constraint. User feedback: "camera is tilting along vertical axis... vertical rotation does not change at all." Fixed by switching to `quat.lookAt(forward, up)` (roll-free by construction) with an empirically-determined negated-forward-vector correction, verified via `rotatedUp === (0,1,0)` exactly and `rotatedForward.y === 0` exactly.
   - **`quat.lookAt` forward-axis sign confusion (recurring)**: had to be re-verified TWICE via numerical debug prints (`multiplyVec3` tests comparing against expected direction) because the convention is non-obvious and got it wrong on a rewrite. Established methodology: never trust the sign without a `[dbgN]` print-and-verify cycle.
   - **Toolbar FILL-mode sizing bug**: user reported text overflowing buttons repeatedly across multiple attempted fixes (padding/size guesses). Finally root-caused by reading `ToolbarLayoutManager.ts` source and adding a strict `forceLayout()` + `computedSize` check, which revealed ALL buttons were clamped to identical `(3.00, 1.19)` regardless of text — a genuine bug in the FILL-mode logic (or my understanding of when it triggers), not a padding/margin miscalculation. Fixed by bypassing FILL mode entirely with explicit MANUAL `size` on each button config.
   - **Vertical menu stacking bottom-up**: user reported "it is following reverse order from bottom to top, it should instead be top to bottom." Root-caused by reading `ToolbarLayoutManager.ts`'s `calculateItemPositions()` (`primaryPaddingStart = padding.bottom` for vertical). Fixed by reversing creation order while preserving index-to-label mapping. Verified via debug print of each item's local Y position (index 0 → y=7.05 top, index 3 → y=-7.05 bottom, confirmed monotonic).
   - **"Doubled text" on blue bottle pack / pink curd tub**: user attributed this to rotation/facing, but investigation (comparing rotation data of g/h against their working siblings d/f — found IDENTICAL rotation values) proved rotation was NOT the cause. Actual cause found by comparing Text3D component properties: `extrusionDepth: 0.4` on g/h vs `0` on all 6 other labels — an authoring inconsistency causing 3D extruded text to show side faces at oblique angles. Fixed via `scene-graphql setProperty` on both Text3D components.
   - **CRITICAL, ONGOING — "camera angle has changed" / "text not appearing for so many products" (the issue currently being resolved as of the last completed action, but which the user has now ESCALATED and asked to be fully reverted rather than further patched)**:
     - I initially misdiagnosed this as caused by the `LABEL_SIZE` 5x change and reverted it (130 restored) — this revert was completed and confirmed clean.
     - I then discovered the REAL contributing factor: `DemoSequencer`'s `autoPlay` was actively running in the background across my rapid testing refreshes (confirmed via query showing `StoreRoot` rotated moments after a fresh refresh), leaving the world in an unexpected rotated state whenever the user or I looked at it. I added a "return to home at the very end of the full sequence" (`panToHomeEnd` phase) as a fix for this.
     - However, the user's LATEST message (received immediately after I completed the extrusionDepth fix and the panToHomeEnd addition) indicates this did NOT resolve their concern and in fact things are WORSE: "text is not appearing for so many products now" (a new/different symptom — functional visibility regression, not just camera framing) and "for some camera was positioning towards them better last time" (camera aiming regression for specific products). The user has now explicitly rejected further incremental patching and demanded a full revert to the pre-LABEL_SIZE-change state for "animation," "camera pointing," and "text logic."

=== USER (2026-08-16T23:15:08.900Z) ===
two issues: white milk pack text appears just after button click and disappear and appears again on camera movement. like it is appearing twice for same thing same time one after other. keep this as in sync with  how it is happening for other products. add expirinf before menu option one and two. expiring tomorrow and expiring this month, replace this week with this month.  do not change anything else without my permission.


--- ASSISTANT (2026-08-16T23:16:06.602Z) ---
Two fixes, both narrowly scoped as requested.

**Milk double-appearance**: root cause is that `ProductTracker.applyLabel()` auto-shows any product matching the filter the instant `setFilter()` fires (this is intentional for real user clicks). `DemoSequencer` was calling `setFilter()` in the `"clicking"` phase, then only hiding matches later in `beginReveal()` (0.5s afterward, in `menuClosing`) — leaving a visible gap where all matches flash on, then off, then back on as the camera arrives at each one. Milk happens to be the first Low-Stock match, so it's the one in view when that flash occurs. Fixing this by hiding matches in the same synchronous step as `setFilter()`, so no frame renders with the stray flash — same fix applies uniformly to every product.

--- ASSISTANT (2026-08-17, appended) ---
Good catch — that's a meaningful behavioral change, not just a bug fix. Read the current DemoSequencer.ts, then made the change:

In the "clicking" phase, removed the code that hid all matches after setFilter() -- ProductTracker's own filter subscription already shows every matching marker/label the instant setFilter() fires, so now all matches for the chosen option appear together immediately, same as a real user would see every matching shelf marker light up at once.

In the panToProduct phase completion, removed the per-arrival setVisible(true) call -- the camera pan now purely visits each marker in turn (like a user looking around), it no longer controls what's visible.

finishOption() was left unchanged -- it still hides all matches together via filterManager.setFilter(NO_FILTER) before the menu reopens for the next option.

Recompiled (succeeded, zero errors) and verified via RunAndCollectLogsTool refresh (clean reset, zero errors). Behavior now: click a filter -> all matching markers/labels appear together immediately -> camera pans between them one by one -> once all have been visited, they disappear together -> menu reopens for the next option.
